- [ ] **Step 3: Migrate `overlay.rs`**

Replace `use machine::Cps1;` with:

```rust
use machine::{Cps1, CpuView};
```

⚠️ `Cps1` stays imported: `draw_sound` still takes one until Task 18.

`executing_pc`:

```rust
/// The address of the instruction about to execute.
///
/// `M68k::pc` after a step points **four bytes past** it, because the core
/// prefetches two words. A breakpoint or a listing keyed on `pc` is therefore one or
/// two instructions late, and for a multi-word instruction it names an address that
/// is not an instruction boundary at all — a breakpoint that "works sometimes".
///
/// Takes the view rather than a board: this is the arithmetic every CPU panel needs
/// and no board is involved in it.
#[must_use]
pub fn executing_pc(v: &CpuView<'_>) -> u32 {
    v.cpu.pc.wrapping_sub(4)
}
```

`draw`:

```rust
/// Draws the enabled panels into an ARGB frame.
///
/// `peek` is how the memory and disassembly panels read the machine. A closure
/// rather than a field on [`CpuView`], because a `&dyn Fn` in the view would make it
/// neither `Copy` nor `Debug` and would hand a vtable to the twenty-odd functions
/// that only want registers. See the memory panel's own doc for why it must be the
/// *side-effect-free* peek and not the CPU's read path.
///
/// ⚠️ `m: &Cps1` is still here for one reason: [`draw_sound`] has not been forked
/// yet. Task 18 replaces that parameter and this note together. Every other panel
/// below reads `v`.
///
/// # Panics
///
/// If `buf` is not a `WIDTH × HEIGHT` frame, as [`draw_text`].
pub fn draw(
    buf: &mut [u32],
    v: &CpuView<'_>,
    peek: &dyn Fn(u32) -> Option<u16>,
    m: &Cps1,
    p: Panels,
    disasm_at: u32,
    mem_at: u32,
    bp: &[u32],
) {
    assert_eq!(buf.len(), WIDTH * HEIGHT, "not a frame");
    if p.regs {
        draw_regs(buf, v);
    }
    if p.disasm {
        draw_disasm(buf, v, peek, disasm_at, bp);
    }
    if p.mem {
        draw_mem(buf, peek, mem_at);
    }
    if p.sound {
        draw_sound(buf, m);
    }
    if p.status {
        draw_status(buf, v);
    }
}
```

⚠️ **`draw_mem` loses its view parameter entirely** — it reads only memory. Do not
keep a `v` it does not use; clippy's `used_underscore_binding` will not catch it but
a reviewer will, and an unused parameter is a claim about a dependency that is not
there.

The four panel functions:

```rust
fn draw_regs(buf: &mut [u32], v: &CpuView<'_>) {
    // …body unchanged, with `m.cpu` → `v.cpu`, `m.total_cycles` → `v.total_cycles`,
    // and `m.board.trace.frames` → `v.trace.frames`.
}

fn draw_disasm(
    buf: &mut [u32],
    v: &CpuView<'_>,
    peek: &dyn Fn(u32) -> Option<u16>,
    at: u32,
    bp: &[u32],
) {
    // …body unchanged, with `m.peek_word(a)` → `peek(a)` and `executing_pc(m)` →
    // `executing_pc(v)`.
}

fn draw_mem(buf: &mut [u32], peek: &dyn Fn(u32) -> Option<u16>, at: u32) {
    // …body unchanged, with `m.peek_word(a)` → `peek(a)`. The `--` branch for `None`
    // stays exactly as it is: see `a_none_from_the_closure_still_prints_dashes`.
}

fn draw_status(buf: &mut [u32], v: &CpuView<'_>) {
    // …body unchanged, with `m.cpu` → `v.cpu`, `m.board.vblank_pending()` →
    // `v.vblank_pending`, and `m.line` → `v.line`.
}
```

⚠️ `draw_status` reads `vblank_pending` as a **field** now, not a call. The value is
identical — [`Machine::cpu_view`] fills it by calling the board's own method — but
the syntax changes, and a mechanical find-and-replace of `m.` → `v.` leaves
`v.vblank_pending()` behind, which does not compile. Good: it fails loudly.

- [ ] **Step 4: Migrate `debug.rs`**

Replace `use machine::Cps1;` with `use machine::CpuView;` — `debug.rs` needs
**no** board type at all after this task, which is the clearest single measure that
the split worked.

Each of the six methods takes `v: &CpuView<'_>` in place of `m: &Cps1`:

```rust
    pub fn update(&mut self, a: &Actions, v: &CpuView<'_>) -> bool {
        // …unchanged, with `executing_pc(m)` → `executing_pc(v)` and
        // `m.cpu.a[7]` → `v.cpu.a[7]`.
    }

    fn scroll(&mut self, v: &CpuView<'_>, forward: bool) { /* executing_pc(v) */ }

    #[must_use]
    pub fn disasm_from(&self, v: &CpuView<'_>) -> u32 {
        self.disasm_at.unwrap_or_else(|| executing_pc(v))
    }

    #[must_use]
    pub fn should_break(&self, v: &CpuView<'_>) -> bool {
        self.stopped_at != Some(v.total_cycles) && self.breakpoints.contains(&executing_pc(v))
    }

    pub fn note_stopped(&mut self, v: &CpuView<'_>) {
        self.stopped_at = Some(v.total_cycles);
    }

    pub fn draw(&self, buf: &mut [u32], v: &CpuView<'_>,
                peek: &dyn Fn(u32) -> Option<u16>, m: &machine::Cps1) {
        if !self.panels.any() {
            return;
        }
        overlay::draw(
            buf,
            v,
            peek,
            m,
            self.panels,
            self.disasm_from(v),
            self.mem_at,
            &self.breakpoints,
        );
    }
```

⚠️ `Debugger::draw` is the **one** method that still names `Cps1`, and only to hand
it through to `draw_sound`. Task 18 deletes that parameter. Every other method in
this file is board-free, and the `use machine::CpuView;` line is what a reviewer
should check: if `Cps1` appears in the import list rather than fully qualified at
this one call, the migration did not go as far as it should.

- [ ] **Step 5: Update `debug.rs`'s and `overlay.rs`'s existing tests**

Both modules' tests build a `Cps1` and pass `&m`. Each call site becomes a view. The
pattern, once per test:

```rust
        // was: dbg.update(&a, &m)
        let mv = machine::Machine::Cps1(Box::new(m));
        dbg.update(&a, &mv.cpu_view());
```

⚠️ **A test that steps the machine between calls cannot hold the `Machine` and the
`Cps1`.** `Machine::Cps1(Box::new(m))` *moves* `m`. Tests that alternate stepping
and drawing must build the `Machine` once and step it through the enum:

```rust
        let mut mv = machine::Machine::Cps1(Box::new(a_machine()));
        mv.step_instruction();
        assert!(dbg.should_break(&mv.cpu_view()));
```

This is the one genuinely fiddly part of the task. Counted: **18 tests in
`debug.rs` and 27 in `overlay.rs`**. All of them are this transformation, and none
of their assertions change. ⚠️ **If an assertion has to change to make a test pass,
stop** — the migration altered behaviour, which it must not. The two files' tests
are the regression net for exactly that.

For the tests that need both a stepping machine and its board (the sound panel's,
which still take `&Cps1`), keep the `Cps1` and build the view from a borrow:

```rust
        // A view over a Cps1 without moving it, for the tests that need both.
        fn view(m: &machine::Cps1) -> machine::CpuView<'_> {
            machine::CpuView {
                cpu: &m.cpu,
                trace: &m.board.trace,
                total_cycles: m.total_cycles,
                line: m.line,
                vblank_pending: m.board.vblank_pending(),
            }
        }
```

⚠️ This helper is `#[cfg(test)]` in `overlay.rs`'s test module and **duplicates
[`Machine::cpu_view`]'s five field assignments.** That duplication is deliberate and
bounded: it exists because `CpuView`'s fields are `pub` (they are — a panel reads
them directly), and because the alternative is moving every test's machine into a
`Machine` even where the test also needs the board. It must not be copied into
non-test code. If a future field is added to `CpuView`, this helper stops compiling,
which is the right failure.

- [ ] **Step 6: Update `loop_.rs`'s call sites**

`run` still takes `&mut Cps1` — the `Machine` conversion is Task 18. So each
debugger call builds a view from the board it already holds. Add near the top of
`loop_.rs`:

```rust
/// A [`CpuView`] over the board this loop is running.
///
/// ⚠️ Temporary, and Task 18 deletes it: once `run` takes a `&mut Machine`, the view
/// comes from [`machine::Machine::cpu_view`] and this duplication goes away. It
/// exists for one task so that the panel migration and the loop's board-generic
/// conversion are two separately reviewable changes.
fn view(m: &Cps1) -> machine::CpuView<'_> {
    machine::CpuView {
        cpu: &m.cpu,
        trace: &m.board.trace,
        total_cycles: m.total_cycles,
        line: m.line,
        vblank_pending: m.board.vblank_pending(),
    }
}
```

and the five call sites become:

```rust
    // was: dbg.update(&a, m)
    dbg.update(&a, &view(m));
    // was: dbg.should_break(m)
    dbg.should_break(&view(m));
    // was: dbg.note_stopped(m)
    dbg.note_stopped(&view(m));
    // was: dbg.draw(&mut buf, m)
    dbg.draw(&mut buf, &view(m), &|a| m.peek_word(a), m);
```

⚠️ **The `peek` closure borrows `m` immutably while `view(m)` also borrows it
immutably** — both fine, two shared borrows. But `dbg.draw` must not be called with
`m` borrowed mutably anywhere in the expression, and the `&mut buf` is a *different*
local, so this compiles. If a future edit makes the loop hold `&mut m` across the
draw, the borrow checker refuses; the fix is to end the mutable borrow before the
draw, never to clone the board.

Run: `cargo test -p frontend`
Expected: PASS — every pre-existing test, unchanged assertions, plus the three new
ones.

Run: `cargo test -p sfemu`
Expected: PASS.

Run: `cargo test --workspace && cargo clippy --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings`
Expected: PASS, clean. ⚠️ Watch for `clippy::too_many_arguments` on
`overlay::draw`, which now takes **eight**. The threshold is seven. Do **not**
`#[allow]` it silently and do not bundle parameters into a struct to dodge it: Task
18 removes the `m: &Cps1` parameter and brings it back to seven. Add
`#[allow(clippy::too_many_arguments)]` with this comment:

```rust
// Eight for one task. `m: &Cps1` is the temporary that Task 18 removes when the
// sound panel forks, which brings this back under the threshold. Bundling the rest
// into a struct to satisfy the lint would outlive the reason for the lint firing.
#[allow(clippy::too_many_arguments)]
```

- [ ] **Step 8: Commit**

```bash
cargo fmt --all && cargo clippy --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings && cargo test --workspace
git add crates/frontend/src/debug.rs crates/frontend/src/overlay.rs crates/sfemu/src/loop_.rs
git commit -m "refactor(frontend): the CPU panels read a CpuView, not a board

debug.rs no longer names a board type at all, and four of overlay.rs's
five panels take a CpuView. Nothing about registers, disassembly, memory
or the status line forks per board: both machines hold an M68k and a
Trace, so all of it is one implementation over five borrowed fields.

Memory arrives as a &dyn Fn(u32) -> Option<u16> rather than a field on
CpuView. A closure in the view would make it neither Copy nor Debug and
would hand a vtable to the twenty-odd functions that only want registers;
four signatures gain a parameter instead of all of them. &dyn rather than
impl Fn because the parameter would otherwise propagate through three
call layers for two indirect calls a frame.

The sound panel is deliberately NOT migrated. It is 200 lines reading one
Z80, one YM and an OKI, against SF1's two Z80s, one YM and two MSM5205s —
a redesign, not a rename, and it forks in the next task. So
overlay::draw still takes a &Cps1 alongside the view, and carries an
allow(too_many_arguments) that the next task removes along with the
parameter. Mixing the rename and the redesign would put both behind one
review, where the rename's noise hides the redesign's decisions.

Three new tests. The executing PC is still four bytes behind the view's
pc, because a prefetching core's pc is not the instruction about to run
and a breakpoint keyed on it fires late. The memory panel reads only
through its closure — asserted by counting the closure's calls, which is
what would catch peek being wired to the wrong board once there are two.
And a None from the closure still prints as dashes rather than FFFF: no
chip here and a chip reading all ones are different facts, and CPS-1's
0x800020 genuinely reads FFFF while being decoded, which is what makes
the distinction real.

No assertion in any pre-existing test changed. That was the constraint:
this task changes what the functions take, not what they draw."
```
